The emblem features a central blue oval containing a white "pelican in her piety," a bird feeding its young with blood from its own breast, which is a traditional symbol of sacrifice and the alchemical process of multiplication <note>In alchemy, the "pelican" refers to a specific type of distilling vessel, while the bird's sacrifice symbolizes the nourishment of the "young" base metals by the "blood" of the philosopher's stone.</note>. The background book cover consists of marbled paper with swirling patterns of purple, tan, and brown.</image-desc>

# HAMBURG /
### Published by Gottfried Liebernickel, 1692.

<summary>This is the title page for the "Gloria Mundi," an alchemical treatise claiming to reveal a secret science passed down from Adam and the Biblical patriarchs. It frames alchemy as a divine gift intended only for the pious and identifies the work as an anonymous contribution to the study of the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>

# Preface.

since the ancients have hidden the **Art** <note>The "Art" refers to alchemy—the sacred science of transforming matter and the soul.</note> with foreign words no less than they have revealed it with proper ones. Therefore, they themselves report that the goal can hardly be reached from their books, unless the reader daily and maturely considers teaching Nature: from what each thing originates, by what means it reaches its <term>final form<note>original: "ultimam speciem" — the perfected or terminal state of a substance in its specific category.</note></term>, and conversely, how it may be brought back from there into its general Mother for a **new birth** <note>original: "neu Geburth" — the alchemical process of purification where a substance is returned to its primal state to be reborn in a higher form.</note>.

It is known to <term>theologians<note>original: "Theologis"</note></term> that man, born of sinful seed and coarse <term>Adamic flesh<note>original: "Adamischen Fleisch" — refers to the fallen, corrupted physical nature of humanity inherited from the biblical Adam, which must be transformed to reach spiritual perfection.</note></term> and blood, cannot possess the Kingdom of Heaven and the Holy Place, unless such a body, with all its lusts and desires, dies away completely,

<meta>catchword: and</meta>

<summary>The author explains that the "Art" of alchemy is intentionally obscured by the ancients and can only be understood by studying the cycles of Nature. He draws a parallel between the alchemical "new birth" and the theological necessity for the physical body to die before entering the Kingdom of Heaven.</summary>

<keywords>The Ancients, The Art, teaching Nature, final form, new birth, theologians, Adamic flesh, spiritual transformation</keywords>

# Preface.

and the <term>seed<note>original: "Saame" — in this context, the vital essence or spark within matter that allows for its growth and transformation.</note></term> of that same body, or its inner essence, be placed again through a holy baptism into the blood of Christ, and thereby be <term>coagulated<note>original: "coagulirt" — the alchemical process of thickening or solidifying a substance; here used metaphorically for the stabilization of the spiritual body.</note></term>, <term>fixed<note>original: "figirt" — from the alchemical term "fixation," meaning to make a volatile or fleeting substance stable and resistant to fire or change.</note></term>, and made permanent for all eternity into a new heavenly and immortal body.

Thus the writings of the prophets and apostles show that the world with its outer bodies may not endure, but the <term>four elements<note>The traditional components of the material world: Earth, Air, Fire, and Water.</note></term> will be melted by the heat, burnt and <term>calcined<note>original: "calcinirt" — to reduce a substance to ash or powder through intense heat; in alchemy, this is the first step of purification.</note></term>, and thereafter we are to expect a new heaven and a new earth.

With this all true <term>philosophers<note>original: "Philosophi" — referring to "natural philosophers" or alchemists who studied the hidden laws of nature.</note></term> agree, in that they set aside the destructible bodies through their own death, and enclose them in a <term>new mother<note>original: "neue Mutter" — a reference to the alchemical vessel or "womb" where a substance is placed to be transformed and "reborn."</note></term> with natural heat for the <term>birthing<note>original: "Gebierung"</note></term> of a new, immortal, clear, and essential body.

<summary>The author draws a parallel between the Christian resurrection and alchemical purification, arguing that both the world and the human body must be "calcined" or destroyed by heat to be reborn in a permanent, immortal form. He asserts that the teachings of the Bible and the secrets of the "philosophers" (alchemists) are in perfect agreement regarding this transformation.</summary>

<keywords>seed, baptism, coagulation, fixation, four elements, calcination, philosophers, alchemical rebirth, new heaven and earth</keywords>

# Preface.

Since the <term>ancients<note>original: "die Alten" — refers to the early masters of alchemy and philosophy who were believed to possess secret knowledge of nature.</note></term> have hidden the <term>Art<note>original: "die Kunst" — alchemy, often called the "Royal Art," which involves both the chemical purification of metals and the spiritual perfection of the soul.</note></term> no less with strange words than they have revealed it with proper ones. Therefore, they themselves also report that the goal can hardly be reached through their books, unless the reader daily and maturely considers <term>nature<note>original: "die lehrende Natur" — nature viewed as the primary instructor or guide in the alchemical process.</note></term> as teacher—namely, from what origin each thing arises, by what means it reaches its <term>final form<note>original: "ultimam speciem" — the ultimate or perfected state of a substance at the end of its transformation.</note></term>, and conversely, how it might be brought back from there into its <term>general mother<note>original: "allgemeine Mutter" — likely a reference to the "first matter" (prima materia) or the alchemical vessel where substances are dissolved to be "reborn."</note></term> for a new birth.

It is known to <term>theologians<note>original: "Theologis"</note></term> that man, [born] of sinful seed and coarse <term>Adamite flesh<note>original: "Adamischen Fleisch" — refers to the fallen, mortal nature of humanity inherited from Adam, which must undergo a symbolic "death" to be purified.</note></term> and blood, cannot possess the Kingdom of Heaven and the Holy Place, unless such a body dies away completely with all its lusts and desires, and

<summary>This page explains that alchemical secrets are intentionally veiled in allegorical language and emphasizes that true understanding comes from observing nature's cycles of birth and dissolution. It compares the alchemical process of "new birth" to the theological requirement for the mortal body to die before entering the Kingdom of Heaven.</summary>

<keywords>alchemy, ancients, secret knowledge, nature as teacher, final form, new birth, theologians, Adamite flesh, transformation</keywords>

# Preface.

and the seed of the same body, or its inward part, be placed again through a holy baptism into the blood of Christ, and thereby be <term>coagulated<note>original: "coagulirt" — in alchemy, the process of turning a liquid substance into a solid, representing the stabilization of spirit into form.</note></term>, <term>fixed<note>original: "figirt" — making a volatile substance stable so it can withstand heat without vanishing.</note></term>, and made permanent for all eternity into a new heavenly and immortal body. # Preface.

for the ancients have hidden the <term>Art<note>original: "Kunst" — here referring to alchemy or the "Great Work."</note></term> no less with strange words as they have revealed it with proper ones. Therefore they themselves also declare that the goal can hardly be reached from their books, unless the reader daily and maturely considers nature as his teacher: from what each thing originates, through what means it arrives at its <term>ultimate form<note>original: "ultimam speciem" — the final state of perfection in an alchemical or natural process.</note></term>, and on the other hand, how it might be brought back from there into its universal mother for a <term>new birth<note>original: "neu Geburth" — a central concept in both alchemy and theology, referring to the spiritual or material regeneration of a substance.</note></term>.

# Preface.

Although now—as previously mentioned—many and almost countless authors have written about such an exceedingly lovely, useful, and magnificent <term>art<note>original: "Kunst" — in this context, the "Art" refers to alchemy or the Hermetic science of understanding and transforming nature.</note></term>, yet so far, in my (modest opinion), none has come to my attention who has brought this matter to light more gracefully, clearly, extensively, and thoroughly than the author of this present little book. 

For besides his clarity, he also settles the dispute where, until now, there has been doubt as to who first discovered this art; for he credibly demonstrates that **GOD** Himself revealed it to the first man, Adam, in Paradise (for which reason the book is also called the *Paradise Table*). 

For since Adam knew the specific character, nature, and effect of every thing, and named all creatures accordingly <note>This refers to the biblical tradition that Adam possessed "perfect knowledge" of nature, allowing him to name the animals according to their true essence.</note>, it could hardly be that anything—

<summary>The author praises the current book for its clarity and argues that the alchemical "Art" is not a human invention, but a divine revelation given by God to Adam in Paradise.</summary>
<keywords>Adam, Paradise Table, divine revelation, origins of alchemy, naming of creatures, Paradise</keywords>

...was hidden from him; therefore Solomon also testifies in the 7th chapter of the Book of Wisdom <note>original: "Sapientiæ" — referring to the deuterocanonical Book of the Wisdom of Solomon.</note> that God gave him certain knowledge of all things, so that he knew how the world was made and understood the power of the elements, etc.

Accordingly, such <term>Philosophy<note>In this context, "Philosophy" refers to the Hermetic or Alchemical philosophy, often called the "Perennial Philosophy," which practitioners believed was the fundamental truth underlying all nature.</note></term> is nothing new; rather, just as Adam received it after his Fall, and as both Moses and Solomon practiced it—because it agrees in every part <note>original: "omni ex parte"</note> and in all its members with **Jesus** (or the Word), it is His likeness, just as He is the image of the Father. And because it also assigns to every philosopher his own saying and explains the same, it indicates their concordance and harmony, in which, according to the statement of **Bernard of Trevisan** <note>original: "Bernhardi Trevisani" — a famous 15th-century alchemist known for his writings on the philosopher's stone.</note>, the truth is certainly to be found.

## Here Follow Several Realizations Regarding Our Stone.

### The first reads as follows.

Son, you should know that our **Stone** is a stone, and may rightly be called a stone; for it is a stone that by nature cannot be called anything else but the <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> <note>original Latin: lapis Philosophorum</note>. No other name is suited to it, for the name corresponds with its nature. I say this: truly it is a stone, and yet not a stone, but for the sake of a likeness we call it a stone. For the **Stone** is so constituted that the **four elements** <term>4 Elementa</term> <note>The fundamental building blocks of Earth, Air, Fire, and Water, which alchemists sought to harmonize.</note> are hidden within it; therefore it has many names and forms, and is

## Another Recognition of our Blessed Stone.

The philosopher **Morienus** <note>Morienus Romanus was a legendary 7th-century hermit and alchemist, traditionally cited as the teacher who brought alchemical wisdom to the Arabic world.</note> speaks and calls our **Stone** a **Water**, in which he then writes no lies: 

O you **Water** in **sour form** <term>saurer Gestalt</term> <note>Likely referring to the "Philosophical Vinegar," a sharp, acidic solvent used to dissolve metals.</note>, you who sustain the **elements**! O you nature of **proximity** <note>original: "Naheit"; likely referring to the internal affinity or sympathetic bond between substances.</note>, you who dissolve nature! O you best of all natures, you who surpass nature! O you greatest of all natures, you who compose the **purification**! O you nature entirely **base** <note>original: "schlimm"; in this context, it refers to the lowly, raw, or "vile" state of the starting material before it is exalted.</note>, you who exalt nature! You are crowned and born with a **light**, and you who contain the **four elements** within yourself, and the **fifth essence** <term>Quinta Essentia</term> <note>The "quintessence" or purest part of a substance, believed to be the celestial element beyond the four earthly ones.</note> has sprung from you! 

At this, the **simple-hearted** <note>original: "Einfältigen"; those who approach the work with humility rather than the "reason" of the learned doctors mentioned on the previous page.</note> rejoice in their simplicity, that nature has received the **composition** <term>Zuhauffsetzung</term> before the composition—unknown to the designs of men—although you yourself bring forth the **mist** <note>original: "Nebel"; often an alchemical metaphor for the vapors produced during heating or the state of "darkness" before the light emerges.</note> and are truly a **mother**; you require no help at all. Therefore, nature contains nature, so that nature is not separated from the **work of nature**; and it is an easy thing.

<summary>This page presents a poetic and paradoxical invocation of the Philosopher's Stone as a "Water" that contains all elements and the Quintessence, stressing that its transformative power is a self-contained work of Nature.</summary>

## The Interpretation Follows.

## Interpretation.

You should know that this is a Salt, as this philosopher Geber says; although in the beginning it does not have the form of a salt, it is nevertheless called a salt, and it is also a salt, black and stinking (for during the work it becomes like clotted blood). Through long duration, it becomes white, pure, bright, and clear; and this is a good, noble salt. Through its own power, it becomes impure and then pure again. It dissolves <note>original: "solvirt"</note> and congeals <note>original: "coagulirt"</note> itself; that is, as the philosopher says, it opens and closes itself. In congealing, it closes the Art <note>The "Art" or "Great Work" refers to the secret process of alchemical transformation.</note>; and in dissolving, it opens the Art. For no other salt may do the same, except only this Salt of the Philosophers.

The Work <note>original: "Handel"; refers to the alchemical operation or the "business" of the Great Work.</note> in this Art <note>original: "Kunst"; the Art of Alchemy.</note> is such a foolish and rejected thing, which so many people seek and yet few find. One finds it clearly stated in the books that the Art is righteous and true in this thing, and it gives forth its Salt <note>original: "Sal"</note>, its entrance. Praised be the eternal God that He has created it! There would be much to write about it according to the sayings of the Sages <note>original: "Sprüchen der Weisen"; the traditional authorities of alchemical literature.</note> who point toward the Salt. 

But I wish to let everyone recognize what they read in the books, and I will write only of an entrance, of its fruit, and of its life: how one should unlock the Garden <note>The "Garden" is a common alchemical metaphor for the vessel or the stage of the process where growth occurs.</note> and see the noble Roses in their field <note>original: "Acker"; literally "acre" or "soil," representing the base material in which the "flowers" of the Work grow.</note>, so that they multiply and bear fruit a thousandfold. And I shall make the dead body <note>original: "todten Cörper"; the "dead" matter or "caput mortuum" left after initial processing.</note> manifest and awaken it to immortal life, so that it can enter into the imperfect bodies <note>In alchemy, base metals like lead or tin were considered "imperfect" or "diseased" versions of gold.</note> to purify them and bring them to complete Perfection, and <term>tincture</term> <note>original: "tingirn"; the final alchemical act where a small amount of the Stone transforms a large amount of base metal into gold.</note> them, so that they also remain eternally constant with it.

<summary>This page describes the alchemical "Work" as a paradox: a rejected, seemingly worthless substance that holds the key to the Art. It uses allegories of a garden and resurrection to explain how the purified Salt can "awaken" dead matter and transform base metals into perfect gold.</summary>

### Now I will write of its entrance, and I will set aside four things, and name it according to three things.

The **Vegetable, Animal, and Mineral Stone** <note>Alchemists often categorized the "Philosopher's Stone" or the materials used to create it into these three kingdoms of nature: Vegetable (plants), Animal (organic matter), and Mineral (metals and earths).</note>, and each one specifically, their effects, with many things; and I will now also set aside these three things, and take for myself one single thing, in which the **four elements** <note>The classical elements: Earth, Air, Fire, and Water.</note> are contained, from which we have our life and origin. ### From Senior regarding Matter and Form: another understanding.
<note>Senior refers to Senior Zadith (Zadith ben Hamuel), a highly influential 10th-century alchemist whose writings were central to medieval and Renaissance hermetic thought.</note>

The Philosopher speaks thus: natural things are those which, from a natural origin—**Matter** and **Form**—are born and produced through Nature. Just as there is a stone and a tree, **Matter** and **Form**, through Nature, are distinct from Nature herself; and that which is composed of these two is called "natural."

The nature of our **Stone**, in its first part, is **Lunaria** <note>original: "Lunaria"; literally "Moon-plant," an alchemical term for the white, silver-like stage of the matter.</note> which has come forth powerfully from a white, **coagulated earth**. ## Another Knowledge of Our Stone

My dear children, you should know that the **Stone** from which our **Art** <note>original: "Kunst"; the sacred practice of alchemy.</note> is worked has not touched the earth of earths, but rather was born before it could do so; and once it has touched the earth, it is no longer fit for our Art. Even though it was sealed by the **Sun** and the **Moon** <note>original: "Solem, und Lunam"; the masculine/active and feminine/passive principles of nature.</note> in its first birth, and its growth is fostered by the earth (since it received its first birth in the earth), it is nonetheless broken, destroyed, and killed. Then the <term>Sun-Son</term> <note>original: "☉ Filius"; the *Filius Philosophorum* or "Son of the Philosophers," a symbol for the perfected Stone resulting from the alchemical marriage of the Sun and Moon.</note> is sealed with a tempered heat through a vapor and is born anew. It comes with a wind into the sea, lying there, and comes with a wind out of the sea onto the land, and passes away immediately <note>original: "vergehet alsbald"; this refers to the volatile nature of the spirit before it is "fixed" or made solid.</note>—

### Regarding the First Matter, or the Seed of Metals, which is enclosed in every Seed; they are two.

For this reason, they must also be nurtured in their own soil <note>original: *Erdreich*</note>, in which they generate and bring forth fruit, like other growing things; and if that were not to happen, no metal would have grown. # A Beautiful Instruction in the True Art.

The Philosophers <note>original: *Philosophi* — the historical alchemical authorities</note> say that no dissolution <note>original: *Auflösung*</note> of the body occurs without the coagulation <note>original: *Coagulirung*</note> of the spirit. As soon as the spirit <note>original: *Spiritus*</note> is transformed into the body <note>original: *Corpus*</note>, it possesses its power. And you should know that as long as the spirit is still volatile <note>original: *flüchtig*</note> and not fixed <term>fixed <note>original: *Fix* — refers to a substance that has been made stable and will no longer evaporate or change when exposed to heat</note></term>, it cannot take effect. As soon as it becomes fixed, however, it accomplishes everything in that very hour that it is meant to accomplish.

Therefore, one must deal with it as the baker deals with bread: take a little of the spirit and give it to the body, just as the baker adds the leaven <term>leaven <note>original: *Sauerteig* — sourdough or yeast; used here as a metaphor for the alchemical "ferment" that triggers the transformation of the whole mixture</note></term> to the flour. The leaven transforms the entire substance <note>original: *Substanz*</note> into a leaven; so too does our spirit, which is our leaven. Thus, one must always ferment <note>original: *fermentiern*</note> until the entire substance becomes a leaven. In this way, the spirit purifies <note>original: *purirt*</note> the body so that it becomes a spirit, and thus the two are transformed—one into the other—

<summary>This page explains the alchemical principle of "fixation," using the metaphor of a baker using leaven to transform dough to illustrate how a small amount of "spirit" can purify and transform the "body" of a metal.</summary>

<keywords>dissolution, coagulation, spirit, body, fixation, leaven, fermentation, purification, alchemy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for distillation begun on the previous page. (56)

After this, place a receiver vessel <note>original: *Fürlag*. A container used to collect the condensed vapors during distillation.</note> in front of it in a <term>Mary’s Bath</term> <note>original: *B. M.* or *Balneum Mariae*. A method of gentle heating using a water bath, named after the legendary alchemist Mary the Jewess.</note> and distill it. Then the water rises up into the receiver, and the salt—which is considered the earth—remains at the bottom or base and becomes dry. In this way, you have separated the moist from the dry. 

Then grind the <term>body</term> <note>original: *Cörper* or *Corpus*. In alchemy, the solid, physical matter remaining after the "spirit" or "soul" has been extracted.</note> small and place it in the <term>Mary’s Bath</term> and let it stand in the heat until it <term>dissolves</term> <note>original: *solvirt*.</note>. Then give it its water back to "drink," all bit by bit, from time to time, until it becomes beautifully clear. For it dissolves and <term>coagulates</term> <note>original: *coagulirt*. To change from a liquid to a solid or semi-solid state.</note> itself; it also purifies itself. For the distilled water is a <term>spirit</term> <note>original: *Spiritus*.</note> that gives the body its life, for it is its only <term>soul</term> <note>original: *Seel*.</note> which is being returned to it.

And that this is true, take note: the water is wind, and the wind is life <note>This reflects the language of the *Tabula Smaragdina* (Emerald Tablet), an ancient alchemical text that describes the creation of the Stone: "The wind carried it in its belly."</note>, and you see that the wind lives, and life is the soul. Therefore, you find water and oil in this work; but the oil remains always with the body and looks like burnt blood. It is purified with the body through the water in the heat over a long period of time, just as you have always been taught herein.

<summary>This page describes the alchemical process of "feeding" a distilled spirit back into a dry solid body to purify it. The author uses the metaphor of a soul returning to a body to explain how the material dissolves and solidifies until it reaches a state of clarity.</summary>
<keywords>distillation, Mary’s Bath, salt, body and soul, spirit, purification, alchemical oil, coagulation</keywords>

Therefore, I wish to give you this entire prescribed summary, until the end of this treatise, as a **testament**, and with it to have diligently warned everyone that you should take heed and protect yourself, so that you undertake nothing in this <term>Art</term> <note>The "Art" (German: *Kunst*) refers to Alchemy or the "Great Work"—the process of transmuting matter and perfecting the soul.</note> unless you first have the right understanding of these things that I have written to you. For there is no thing in the whole world from which the Art is worked except for this one single thing; whoever does not know or recognize this will never come to the Art.

For it is a thing that one does not get out of mountains or pits of the earth, like <term>Gold</term> <note>original: *Sol* (the Sun)</note>, <term>Silver</term> <note>original: *Luna* (the Moon)</note>, <term>Sulfur</term>, <term>Salt</term>, <term>Mercury</term> <note>indicated by the symbol ☿</note>, <term>Copper</term> <note>indicated by the symbol ♀ (Venus)</note>, <term>Gold</term> <note>indicated by the symbol ☉ (the Sun)</note>, and the like, as GOD has created them. This is all nothing; rather, it is a thing that comes out of a quite dark ore, revealed to the light. And as soon as it falls from its earth, it rumbles and lets itself be seen openly; and as soon as it touches the ground, it disappears immediately and is no longer seen or found. Rather, it must be caught in the air during its fall, before it touches the ground, and work[ed]—

<summary>The author presents a "testament" warning that the alchemical Art requires one specific, secret substance. This substance is not a common mineral or metal but a mysterious matter that must be "caught from the air" before it touches the earth.</summary>

It is also called the Holy Unity—understand this to mean the reconciliation of the opposing elements that are within it. It is the **Philosopher’s Stone** <note>original: *Stein der Philosophorum*</note>: the stone of the Sun, of the metals, the **fugitive servant** <note>original: *der flüchtige Knecht*; a common alchemical nickname for Mercury or the volatile spirit that "runs away" unless fixed.</note>, the ore-like stone, the animal stone <note>original: *Thirnische Stein*, likely a corruption of *Thierische*, referring to the "Animal Stone" in the triad of Mineral, Vegetable, and Animal stones.</note>, the special stone. It is also called an origin of worldly things. It is also called **Xelis**, which is pebble <note>original: *silex*</note> reversed; **Xidar**, which is root <note>original: *radix*</note> reversed; **Petra**, a rock; **Atrop**, which is gate <note>original: *porta*</note> reversed. And because it is a common thing, I want to have warned you all, that you beware of other common sophistical <note>In this context, "sophistical" refers to "sophistic" alchemy—deceptive, false practices or "puffers" who use incorrect materials like common lead or salts.</note>...

<term>Hyle</term> <note>original: *Hyle*; from the Greek word for "wood" or "matter," used by alchemists to describe the raw, undifferentiated substance of the universe.</note> is the first matter, and the <term>Salt of the Philosophers</term> <note>original: *Saltz der Philoſophorum*; a symbolic mineral principle representing the "body" or the fixed part of the alchemical work.</note>: the <term>Azoth</term> <note>original: *Azoth*; believed by alchemists to be the universal solvent or the "mercury" of the sages, often considered the transformative spirit of the work.</note>, and the seed of all metals, which is drawn out from the body of <term>Magnesia</term> <note>original: *Magneſiæ*; in alchemy, this refers to a crude mineral ore containing the potential for the philosopher's stone, rather than modern magnesium.</note> and <term>Lunaria</term> <note>original: *Lunariæ*; literally "of the moon," referring to the silvery, feminine, or cooling principle in the process.</note>.

**Hyle** is a beginning of all things, and in the beginning there was a matter; the same was not moist, nor dry; it was not earth, nor water; it was not light, nor dark; not air, nor fire; but it was a confused mixture, which is **Hyle**.

# The Second Part of this Book Follows.

Firstly, it is to be understood that when **GOD** the Almighty, in the beginning, placed [him] together with all heavenly and earthly bodies and our first father <term>Adam</term> <note>In alchemical and theological texts, Adam represents the "perfect man" before his physical and spiritual degradation.</note> into <term>Paradise</term> <note>original: *Paradiess*; the Garden of Eden, seen here as a state of primordial harmony.</note>, and forbade him from eating the <term>fruit of the tree</term> <note>original: *Frucht des Baums*.</note>—namely the **apple**—under penalty of eternal death; at that same time, before Adam transgressed the commandment, man had no need for any earthly thing, seeing as man was still **immortal** at that time. 

But as soon as he had fallen by eating the forbidden fruit of the <term>knowledge of good and evil</term> <note>original: *Erkanntniß guts und böß*; referring to the biblical Fall of Man which, in the alchemist's view, introduced corruption and disease into the human condition.</note>, he immediately became in need of all things in the world, after he was driven by the **Angel**, at the command of **God**, out of Paradise and into <term>misery</term> <note>original: *Elend*; in early modern German, this word often carried the specific meaning of "exile" or being in a foreign land, as well as general suffering.</note>—that is, into this sorrowful world—and nothing else

<summary>This page marks the beginning of the Second Part, explaining that before the Fall, Adam was immortal and lacked nothing, but his exile from Paradise into the "sorrowful world" created human necessity and suffering.</summary>

## To the Reader.

So that you, reader, are not long delayed, but may understand my meaning and the basis <note>original: *Grund*; the fundamental principles or "grounding" of the alchemical art.</note> [of this work] as quickly and efficiently as possible, and do not become weary in reading, pay diligent attention to what I have revealed and named for you in this treatise—in the beginning, middle, and end. ## Now follows the Origin of the Metals.

Dear son, here I want to indicate and show you the origin of the metals, from whence they have taken their beginning and form; for afterwards you shall have a thorough understanding. winter retreats, then the flowers of the field perish, and the leaves fall from the trees; all herbs wither, because the **element of fire** <note>In alchemical thought, "fire" refers to the vital heat or "sulfur" that gives life and growth to all things.</note> departs from them. <summary>The author explains that the balance of heat (Sun) and cold (Moon) is essential for growth, and argues that alchemy can use a "natural fire" to instantly achieve the perfection of metals that would otherwise take nature a thousand years.</summary>

To that end, I will give you an example: You have common **Sulfur** <term>Sulphur</term> <note>original: *Sulphur*; in alchemy, sulfur represents the "soul," the active, combustible, and masculine principle of a substance.</note> which **coagulates** <note>To turn from a liquid to a solid state.</note> common **Mercury** <term>Mercurius</term> <note>original: *Merc.*; mercury represents the "spirit," the feminine, fluid, and metallic principle.</note>, as you see in the work <note>Refers to the "Great Work" (*Magnum Opus*) of alchemical transformation.</note> and in other things besides. For this Sulfur is poisonous and the Mercury is deadly; how then should you extract something good from them that might come to the aid of another thing? That is hardly possible, for they both themselves require help to reach **perfection** <note>original: *Vollkommenheit*; the stable, purified state, often equated with gold or the Elixir.</note>.

But I tell you truly: if you have united our **fixed Sulfur** <note>Sulfur that has been processed so it no longer evaporates when heated.</note> with our **sublimated Mercury** <note>Mercury purified through the process of sublimation—turning it into vapor and back to solid—to remove impurities.</note>, and both are made to stand in **fire** <term>Feur</term>, then cast a little of that onto **raw mercury** <term>Merc. crudum</term> <note>Common quicksilver found in nature.</note>. Then it transforms itself and becomes perfect, and remains perfect. 

Now return again to the previous words regarding the **Fifth Essence** <term>Quinta Essentia</term> <note>original: *Q. E.*; the "quintessence" or highest, most medicinal part of a substance, beyond the four elements of earth, water, air, and fire.</note> of the earth and the **Fifth Essence of the Stars** <term>Q. E. Stellarum</term> <note>original: *Q. E. Stellarum*; the celestial or "astral" energy believed to descend from the heavens to give life to minerals and plants.</note>. You see clearly that the **Fifth Essence** of the earth has its effect in winter, when the soil is closed with frost; and the **Fifth Essence of the Stars** has its effect on the...

# Third Treatise
## Concerning the Assembly of the Philosophers

<margin>1.</margin> Which I wish to explain word for word, with all points and articles, in particular, so that every reader may understand everything that the **philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophi*; the historical masters of alchemy such as Hermes Trismegistus or Geber.</note> write, in order that he may proceed to the work more quickly and surely; thereby praise God the Lord and wish me His grace at the beginning of my undertaking to complete [it], so that you, reader, might no longer be delayed, but rather come to the true understanding of this our **art** <note>original: *Kunst*; a standard term for the "Great Work" or the practice of alchemy.</note>, just as these prepared philosophers [did]. So you should now know that Almighty God himself first gave this art to our father Adam in Paradise; for as soon as He had created him and [placed him] into the...

<summary>This page marks the beginning of the Third Treatise, moving from technical instructions to the spiritual history of alchemy. The author asserts that the secret knowledge of the "Art" was a divine gift bestowed directly by God upon Adam in the Garden of Eden.</summary>

<keywords>Third Treatise, assembly of philosophers, divine origin of alchemy, Adam, Paradise, virtue, the Art</keywords>

<meta>catchword: The</meta>

Paradise, there He gave him this **art** <note>original: *Kunst*; referring to the alchemical craft.</note>, speaking thus: "Adam, see, there are two things: that which is upwards is **volatile** <note>original: *flüchtig*; in alchemy, substances that evaporate or change state easily when heated.</note>, and that which is downwards is **fixed** <note>original: *Fix*; substances that resist the fire and remain stable.</note>. In these two, all mystery is hidden and locked away; hold this in high regard. This secret power and **virtue** <note>original: *Tugend*; the inherent transformative or medicinal power of a substance.</note> that lies within them, keep hidden from all your sons and descendants. These two, together with all other created things under heaven, shall serve you and be obedient to you; and I will have all their power and the glory of the whole world subjected to you. Therefore, you are compared to the whole world and called the <term>microcosm</term> <note>original: *minor Mundus*; Latin for "smaller world." This is the Hermetic belief that the human being is a miniature map of the entire universe.</note>, which is the little world." Thus ends this speech made by the Creator to our first father Adam, concerning the high mystery of **philosophy** <note>original: *Philosophia*; here meaning the "True Philosophy" or the Great Work of alchemy.</note>.

# The Saying of Abel.

<margin>2.</margin>Abel, the son of Adam, wrote in his beginning: When God created our father Adam and placed him in Paradise, He cast all things—herbs, mountains, stones, and animals which are not named—into our father Adam, so that he should be a Lord of all things. From this, Nature brought it about that man should be a mountain of all mountains, a stone of all stones, a tree of all trees, a herb of all herbs, a root of all roots, and an earth of all earths. He may be named and regarded as such because all the powers of all things are preserved within man; this is what God has given him.

3. Seth, the son of Adam, describes it to us thus: "You children, you shall know that however much **vinegar** <note>original: *Essig*; in alchemy, this usually refers to a sharp, acidic solvent or "philosophical vinegar" used to dissolve substances.</note> is boiled down in the **Art** <note>original: *Kunst*; the alchemical practice.</note> and turned to ashes, so many times does it bring forth and make the **body** <note>original: *Corpus*; the physical substance being worked upon in the flask.</note> white. If you simmer or cook it well and rob it of its **blackness** <note>original: *Schwertß*, likely a variant of *Schwärze*; refers to the *nigredo* or "black stage," the initial phase of decay and purification in the Great Work.</note>, then it transforms itself into the **Stone** <note>original: *Stein*; the Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the alchemical process.</note>."

<summary>This page features teachings attributed to Adam's sons; Abel explains that man is a microcosm containing the essence of all nature, while Seth provides a cryptic recipe for the Philosopher's Stone involving the whitening of a "body" using philosophical vinegar.</summary>

<keywords>Abel, Seth, microcosm, Adam, philosophical vinegar, whitening, nigredo, Philosopher's Stone, creation</keywords>

...and it is called the **white stone** <note>original: *weiße Stein*; the second major stage of the Great Work, associated with the moon and silver.</note> for as long as it is broken; and dissolve it in the **Water of the Moon** <note>original: *Wasser des Monds*; likely a silver-based solvent or a purified, cooling mercurial liquid.</note> well tempered. 6. **Senior**, otherwise known as **Pandolphus** <note>Senior Zadith filius Hamuel and Pandolphus are authorities frequently cited in the "Turba Philosophorum," an influential early alchemical text.</note>, I make it known to posterity that the thinness of the **Air** is within the **Water**, and it is not separated from the others; and if the **Earth** were not dry upon the Water, no **moisture** <note>original: *Feuchtigkeit*.</note> would remain.

In seeing, one recognizes the difference between white and black; in hearing, one recognizes the integrity <note>original: *Redligkeit*; referring to the truth or quality of the sound.</note> of the good and the evil.

<meta>This page continues the discourse of various philosophers—Blodius, Lea, Alkius, and Bonellus—describing the nature and origin of the "Stone" and the necessity of focusing on a single, unified substance rather than complex, multi-stage processes.</meta>

(126)

31. **Blodius**, Take the stone / that is found in all places / and is called **Rebis** <term>original: *Rebis*; from the Latin *res bina*, meaning "a double thing." It represents the alchemical hermaphrodite or the union of opposites like spirit and body.</term>, and grows in two mountains / and take it alone while fresh / with its own blood / and its increase is in the skin / as well as in the flesh / and its nourishment is in the blood / and it dwells in the air / so take as much of it as you will / and bring it to the **bath** <note>original: *Bad*; refers to the *balneum*, a method of gentle, indirect heating (like a double boiler) used to facilitate chemical or spiritual transformation.</note>.

32. **Lea, the Prophetess** <note>A female authority figure often cited in the *Turba Philosophorum*; her presence highlights the historical role of women in the alchemical tradition.</note> / describes it to us in few words / thus / you, **Nathan** <note>An interlocutor or student frequently addressed in these dialogues.</note>, shall know / that the **flower of gold** <note>original: *Blumen des Golds*; a term for the purest essence or the initial "bloom" of the completed work.</note> is a stone / Therefore roast it for several days / until it becomes like a shining marble stone.

33. **Alkius**, Every day you see the mountains standing before you / that is man and woman / so go to their pits / and dig / and fetch from there their earths / before they perish.

# Count Bernard <note>Bernhardus Trevisanus, a 15th-century Italian alchemist whose works emphasize the use of a single, unified substance for the Great Work.</note>

<margin>57.</margin>**Geber** <note>The Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan, an 8th-century polymath; his Latin works were the bedrock of Western alchemy.</note>, we have not been able to find / a thing that remains and endures in the fire / except only the natural **viscous humidity** <term>original: *viscosa humiditas*; a thick, oily moisture thought to be the binding force of matter that does not evaporate when heated.</term> / which is the root of all metals / for the worthy Stone / one needs nothing more than the **Mercurial substances** <term>Refers to the philosophical principle of "Mercury"—the fluid, transformative essence of matter—rather than common quicksilver.</term> / well purified by art, penetrating / coloring / persisting in the fire / standing in the battle of the fire / not abandoning itself / in the monarchies <note>Likely referring to the 'Four Monarchies' or kingdoms of elemental and planetary influence.</note> / but always keeping itself in its Mercurial essence / that is a thing / which unites itself at the base of the root with the metal / and breaks their imperfect form / and leads them into another form / according to the power of the **Elixir** / or **Medicine** <note>original: *Medicinæ*; in alchemy, the "Medicine" is the agent that "heals" base metals into gold.</note>.

<margin>58.</margin>**Aros** <note>A figure often cited in alchemical dialogues like the *Turba Philosophorum*; sometimes associated with Mary the Prophetess.</note>, our Medicine is made / of two things of one essence / from the unity of the Mercurial, **Fixed** <term>Stable and resistant to the heat of the fire.</term> / and **Not Fixed** <term>Volatile or prone to evaporating when heated.</term> / spiritual / physical / cold / and moist / hot and dry / and from other things / it cannot be made.

## The interpretation of the saying of Adam reads as follows.

When **God the Almighty** created our first father **Adam** and placed him in **Paradise**, he showed him two things: **Earth and Water**. The Earth is **fixed** <term>original: *Fix*; in alchemy, "fixed" substances are those that can withstand fire without evaporating or changing.</term> and constant; the Water is **volatile** <term>original: *flüchtig*; refers to substances that easily evaporate or change state when heated.</term> and inconstant. And in these two are hidden all manner of things that **God** created. Thus, within the Water dwells the **Air**, and within the Earth—through the **creation and order of God**—dwell the stones and the **Fire**. And from these **four elements**, all things spring forth, both in heaven and on earth.

## The Interpretation of the Saying of Abel, etc.

This saying almost explains itself; it is also sufficiently reported in the words of **GOD** and the saying of **Adam**. But according to my opinion and knowledge, I say this: that man possesses within himself all the powers of those things, as this saying clearly implies; that man is considered a **small world** <note>original: *kleine Welt*; this refers to the "Microcosm," the ancient belief that the human body is a miniature reflection of the entire universe (the Macrocosm).</note> and compares himself entirely to the world. For his bones within the flesh are compared to stones and mountains, by which the **body** <note>original: *Corpus*</note> is made firm, just as the earth is with stones; and the flesh is accounted for the earth, and the great veins for the great rivers or

## The Interpretation of the Saying of Seth, the Son of Adam.

The **Philosopher Seth** <note>Seth, the third son of Adam, was traditionally revered in alchemical lore as a primary recipient of antediluvian wisdom and the "Art" of chemistry.</note> says: "as much vinegar as is boiled away / and turned to ashes." By this he means that as much of the **distilled water**—which we call <term>seed</term> <note>original: *Sperma*; here referring to the "mercurial water" or the vital, life-giving essence of the matter rather than a biological fluid.</note>—as is turned into a <term>body</term> <note>original: *Corpus*; the solid, fixed material result of the process.</note> through long boiling (which he calls "ashes"), so much does it draw blackness out from the body and wash it white. For the distilled water transforms itself through the means of long boiling into a body, and all sorts of things rise up during the work / and drive away the stench and blackness from the earth. And you should know / that if the earth were not present / the <term>spirit</term> <note>original: *Spiritus*; the volatile, gaseous, or active essence of a substance.</note> could not be <term>coagulated</term> <note>original: *coagulirt*; the alchemical process of "fixing" a volatile or liquid substance into a stable solid form.</note> / for it would have no <term>receptacle</term> <note>original: *Receptacel*; a vessel or physical host required to "catch" and hold the elusive spirit.</note> in which it could be contained / nor can it be coagulated in any other thing—

<summary>This page presents an interpretation of a teaching by Seth, explaining how distilled "mercurial water" is transformed into a solid "body" through prolonged boiling. It emphasizes the role of "earth" as the necessary vessel to catch and solidify the volatile spirit, a process that purifies the matter from its initial "blackness."</summary>
<keywords>Seth, Adam, distillation, seed, coagulation, spirit and body, purification, alchemical earth</keywords>

(157)

[to be attained] / for in its own <term>spirit</term> <note>original: *Spirit*; the volatile, active essence of the substance.</note>, it shall be purified and bound; therefore the spirit purifies its own <term>body</term> <note>original: *Corpus*; the solid, physical matter.</note>, as **Seth the Philosopher** says: "it makes the body white," and he speaks truly. 

Furthermore, the Philosopher says: if you boil it well and rob it of its blackness / then it turns into a stone / and is called the **"white stone penny."** <note>The "white stone penny" is a metaphor for the White Work (*albedo*), the stage of the alchemical process where the matter is purified and attains the power to transmute metals into silver and grant health.</note> By this, the Philosopher means / that one should boil it well / gently / and slowly / and not too hot / thus, over time, it transforms itself into a <term>body</term> that remains stable in the fire / and is called a "stone" / because it is permanent / and it acquires a white color / in the whitest manner / and it is called a "penny." 

And this is the reason: just as he who has a penny / for which one may receive beer and bread / he receives for it whatever he desires / **Health** / **Wisdom** / **Long Life** / <term>Sol</term> <note>Gold (the Sun).</note>, <term>Luna</term> <note>Silver (the Moon).</note>, pearls / precious stones. Therefore it is rightly called a penny / and everything that one cannot get for other pennies / or for all the goods of the world / that one receives through this "penny."

<summary>This page explains the "White Work" of alchemy, where the matter is purified through gentle boiling into a stable "white stone." This stone is compared to a universal "penny" because it can be traded for health, wisdom, and precious metals.</summary>

It is also called a penny because it is struck by the **Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophis*; referring to the alchemical masters of the past.</note> themselves; for they have struck their own sign or image into the penny, just as kings and princes strike their sign and image upon their coins.

Furthermore, as the Philosopher says: boil the **stone** until it breaks, and <term>dissolve</term> <note>original: *solvirt*; from the Latin *solvere*, to loosen or melt into liquid.</note> it in the **Water of the Moon** <note>In this context, the "Water of the Moon" refers to a specific cooling solvent or a state of the matter during the "whitening" stage of the work.</note>, well-tempered.

By this he means that one should boil the stone in itself until it turns into water—that is, until it is dissolved. This happens through its own power, for the **body** <note>original: *Corpus*; the solid matter.</note> is called the **Moon** as soon as it is changed into water; and the drawn-off **spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus*; the volatile essence.</note>, acting as the distilled water, is called the **Sun**, because the element of air is hidden within it. The **Art** <term>original German: *Kunst*; a standard term for the "Great Work" of alchemy.</term> consists of thinness and thickness. These two must be joined together with a tempered warmth—not too hot, nor too cold—so that Nature is not scorched. This represents **Winter** and **Summer**; and when Winter and Summer come together, a tempered warmth is produced, as in a natural bath <note>Likely referring to a *balneum Mariae* (water bath), which provides the gentle, steady heat necessary for the "incubation" of the alchemical stone.</note>. Such tempered warmth gives rise to the **Sun** <note>original Latin: *Solem*; in this context, signifying gold or the active, masculine principle.</note> and the **Moon** <note>original Latin: *Lunam*; signifying silver or the passive, feminine principle.</note>. Therefore, our philosopher **Isidore** <note>Likely Isidore of Seville (c. 560–636), an influential scholar whose encyclopedic works were frequently cited by later alchemists to provide authority to their natural philosophy.</note> provides this parable, which I have interpreted according to God’s right, provided you pay attention with true understanding.

# Explanation of the Saying of
->Pythagoras.<-

---

The Philosopher <note>likely referring to Pythagoras himself, or "The Philosopher" as a generic title for an ancient authority</note> asks what that may be which one feels, and does not see, and does not know. By this, the Philosopher means it is a thing of our Art <note>original: *Kunſt*; "The Art" is the standard historical term for Alchemy</note>, which is prepared through our Art, and it is a thing that one feels and does not see. This is to be understood thus: one feels it, but one does not see it; however, one does not know its effect. But whoever knows the thing but does not know its effect shall never arrive at the Art. For there is nothing more than one single thing in the entire world from which the Art is made and worked. And that thing comes from a dark place where it is never seen, and its effect and virtue <note>original: *Tugend*; refers to the inherent power, potency, or medicinal property of a substance</note> are recognized by no one except by the wise of this Art alone. Also, the great secret is hidden within the matter <note>original: *Materia*</note>, such as the air and the fire—that is, the Sun and the Moon <note>original Latin: *Sol* and *Luna*; in alchemy, these represent the masculine/gold and feminine/silver principles respectively</note>.

<summary>This page presents an alchemical riddle attributed to Pythagoras concerning the "Prima Materia"—the single, hidden substance found in a dark place that serves as the sole basis for the alchemical work.</summary>
<keywords>Pythagoras, Alchemical Art, Prima Materia, Sun and Moon, Sol and Luna, occult qualities, four elements</keywords>

<meta>This page transitions from the physical description of the "Earth of the Philosophers" on page 183 to a philosophical defense of the alchemical tradition. It introduces an interpretation of Pythagoras to explain that the apparent contradictions in alchemical texts are due to the reader's misunderstanding rather than the authors' deception.</meta>

(184)

## Interpretation of the Saying of Pythagoras.

You may well marvel at the strange and wondrous sayings of the **Philosophers**, and how manifoldly they have described this thing; yet they agree on one thing, and all their sayings proceed from a single point. If you otherwise pay close attention, you will experience the truth, as the **Philosopher** <note>In alchemical texts, "The Philosopher" often refers to Aristotle, but here it likely refers to Pythagoras or the collective authority of the masters.</note> says: "We give you a rule <note>original: *Regiment*; meaning a system of direction or a method of procedure</note> concerning these things: let it be **Water**, dry as the **Water of the Moon** <note>original: *Wasser des Monds*; a classic alchemical paradox referring to a substance—often a form of Mercury—that behaves like a fluid but does not wet the hands.</note> which you have prepared." By this, the **Philosopher** means that if we wish to work in this **Art** <note>The "Art" is the standard term for the practice of alchemy.</note>, we should govern ourselves strictly according to true understanding. If we then fail and do not hit the mark, we might easily scold the masters for being deceptive; for they provide the rule openly. However, if you do not succeed, it is only your own [supposed] cleverness or your folly that is to blame, because you interpret the **Scripture** <note>original: *Schrifft*; here referring to the authoritative alchemical manuscripts and books.</note> according to your own mad heads in a way other than intended.

->For<-

<summary>This page introduces an interpretation of Pythagoras, arguing that the "Art" of alchemy is described consistently by all true philosophers. It warns the reader that failure in the work stems from their own faulty interpretation of the "dry Water of the Moon" rather than from any deception by the authors.</summary>

<keywords>Pythagoras, Water of the Moon, Alchemical Art, Rule of the Work, Interpretation of Scripture, Philosophers</keywords>

For it reads: Further than what the **Philosopher** <note>original: *Philosophus*; likely referring to Pythagoras, as mentioned on the previous page.</note> says, that it should drink its own water. By this he means that when the dry has been separated from the moist, then the drawn-off water of the **Body** <note>original: *Corpus*; the solid substance or material residue in the alchemical vessel.</note> is true water, and is the **Water of the Moon** <note>original: *Wasser des Monds*; a symbolic term for a purified, silvery liquid, often associated with philosophical mercury.</note> that you have prepared through **putrefaction** <note>original: *putrifaction*; the stage of the process where matter is broken down and decomposed, usually resulting in a black color.</note> and **distillation**. For the drawn-off water is accounted as the **Man**, and the earth as the **Body** is for the **Woman**. When he has become water within himself, then you must add the water of the **Man** to the water of the **Woman** completely, so that they bind themselves together in **Marriage** <note>original: *Eheschafft*; alchemy frequently uses the "chemical wedding" as a metaphor for the union of volatile and fixed substances.</note>. Thus the **Body** drinks its own prepared water from time to time and becomes ever purer; and it drinks no more, for so long, until it becomes white beyond all measure. That is then called our **Lime** <note>original: *Kalck*; referring to a *calx*, the fine powder produced after a substance has been heated or "calcined."</note>. And pour the water of our **Lime** onto the **Body**, and let it dry in through natural heat. Let that happen so often and many times until it colors itself and becomes the whitest of all. Then it is prepared for the white stage, and

This repeated cycle of moistening and drying leads to the creation of a "white lime," a crucial stage in the alchemical Great Work.</summary>

...has drunk its own **moisture** <note>original: *Feuchtigkeit*; in this context, the refined liquid or "mercury" that the solid substance has reabsorbed.</note>. But if you wish to bring it further to the **red** <note>original: *roth*; referring to the *Rubedo*, the final stage of the alchemical Great Work, which results in the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, then treat it once more with **dissolving** <note>original: *solvirn*; to break down a solid into a liquid state.</note> and **coagulating** <note>original: *coagulirn*; to thicken a liquid back into a solid state.</note>, just as you did with the **white** <note>original: *Weissen*; the *Albedo* or "white" stage, which represents a high level of purity before the final reddening.</note>, in every respect and not otherwise. And this is truly, most certainly true, plain and righteous, according to the proper work of the **Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophen*; refers to the ancient and medieval masters of alchemy.</note>, etc.

## Interpretation and Meaning of the Emerald Tablet of Hermes

When **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary father of Hermeticism and alchemy.</note> says that the **Art** <note>original: *Kunst*; a common term for alchemy, often called the "Royal Art."</note> is true and most certainly truthful, he means that the Art is correctly handed down by the **Philosophers**. However, the unwise interpret the writings differently than they are written, and they understand them in a way other than they should. When they then fail and do not succeed, they say the Art is not true, that the **Philosophers** have deceived them and written lies; they then disparage the Art, claiming it is not true. Thus, those who work are despised along with the Art; but no one is to blame <note>The catchword at the bottom of the page indicates the next page likely continues: "...[but their own ignorance].</note>

<summary>This page provides instructions for progressing from the "white" stage to the "red" stage of alchemy through repeated cycles of dissolution and coagulation. It then begins a defense of the Alchemical Art, citing the Emerald Tablet and blaming the failures of practitioners on their own misinterpretations rather than the teachings of the masters.</summary>

<keywords>Rubedo, Albedo, Solve et Coagula, Hermes Trismegistus, Emerald Tablet, Alchemical Art, Philosophers, moisture, dissolution, coagulation</keywords>

--- Page 245 ---
<meta>This page continues the interpretation of the Emerald Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus, shifting from a defense of alchemical truth to a practical-symbolic explanation of the relationship between "Above" and "Below."</meta>

(187)

...fault of the unwise fools who do not understand the **Scripture** <note>original: *Schrifft*; referring here to the sacred Hermetic texts or alchemical writings.</note>; for if they had looked at the **Art** <note>original: *Kunst*; alchemy, often called the "Royal Art."</note> correctly, read it with understanding, and governed themselves accordingly, and had not followed their own mad heads and their own understanding, they would not have failed. But since they err, it is their own fault and not the fault of the **Philosophers** <note>The "Philosophers" are the ancient and medieval masters of alchemy whose works were considered authoritative.</note>; for they have written the righteous truth, and the **Art** is true and not a lie.

Furthermore, when he <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary author of the Emerald Tablet.</note> says that that which is **Upwards** is also that which is **Downwards** <note>This is a commentary on the famous Hermetic maxim: "That which is below is like that which is above, and that which is above is like that which is below."</note>, by this he means the **Matter** <note>original: *Mat.* / *Materia*.</note> of our **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the ultimate goal of the alchemical process.</note> is one thing at the beginning of our **Work**. And that one thing is divided into two parts, namely into **Earth** and **Water**. And the **Water** that rises up is **volatile** <note>original: *flüchtig*; referring to a substance that easily evaporates or transitions into a gaseous state.</note>, and the **Earth** that remains below is **Fixed** <note>original: *Fix*; referring to a substance that is stable, solid, and resistant to the fire.</note>. And when they are joined together again, the **Body** <note>original: *Corpus*.</note> becomes a **Spirit**, and the **Spirit** becomes a **Body**; just as the **Earth** becomes **volatile**, for it transforms into **Water**, and the **Water** becomes **Fixed**, for it transforms into a **Body**. As the **Philosophers** say: when the **Bodies** [become] **Spirits**, and the...

<summary>This page defends the validity of alchemy against those who fail through their own misunderstanding and provides a chemical interpretation of the Emerald Tablet's "Above and Below" as the separation and reunion of volatile water and fixed earth.</summary>

The **Body** <term>Corpus <gloss>Latin: Body. In alchemy, the solid or material aspect of the substance.</gloss></term> is born anew, and grows and increases like a fruit in the mother’s womb; and thus our **Stone** is born from the **Mass** <term>Massa <gloss>Latin: The unrefined, original "lump" of matter being worked upon.</gloss></term>, which encloses all four **Elements** within itself. Since it is naturally born from two parts—the **Body** <note>original: "Corp." for *Corpus*</note> and the **Spirit** <term>Spiritus <gloss>Latin: Spirit. Thus, the **Stone** receives the **Spirit** <note>original: "Spiritum"</note> from above from **Heaven**, and from below from the **Earth**, and so becomes a newborn fruit, in the very same way that all fruits are conceived in the mother’s womb. And just as all creatures bear fruit, so also does the **Air** or **Wind** give birth to our **Stone**, and not otherwise.

Furthermore, as **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom the foundation of alchemy is attributed.</note> says: its power is complete when it is changed into **Earth**. By this he means: as soon as the **Spirit** is transformed into a **Body**, then it has its power. For you must understand that while the **Spirit** is still volatile <note>original: "flüchtig." In alchemy, a volatile substance is one that evaporates or escapes easily when heated.</note> and not yet **Fixed** <term>Fix <gloss>A "fixed" substance is stable in fire and does not evaporate.</gloss></term> and made permanent, it performs in that moment everything it is meant to perform; and once it has reached that state...

The solid or material aspect of the substance.</gloss></term>, just as the baker adds the sourdough <note>The author uses the analogy of leavening bread to explain "fermentation," a process where a small amount of the perfected "Stone" transforms a larger mass of base metal.</note> to the flour. By this, the **Stone** rises from the Earth into Heaven, and from Heaven back to the Earth, and takes to itself the power from above and from below. Thus you have the glory and clarity of the whole world; therefore all poverty, sickness, and darkness will flee from you, when it overcomes **Mercury** <note>original: "Merc." for Mercurius. By this **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus.</note> means that when one wishes to begin the work, one should separate the moist from the dry, such as the Water from the Earth; for the fire rises above from the Water and [perfects] the **Body** <note>original: "völlig den Corp." when it is given to it—that is the **Fire**—separated from the Earth, just as the subtle is separated from the hard. The **Water** is subtle; the **Earth**, being the **Body** <term>Body <gloss>Latin: Corpus. The solid or material aspect of the substance.</gloss></term>, is hard and coarse. This should happen visibly with great skill; that is, in the **Dung of the Philosophers** <term>Dung of the Philosophers <gloss>Latin: fimus equinus. A traditional alchemical heat source created by a pile of fermenting horse manure, which provides a steady, gentle warmth.</gloss></term>, or in a **Bath** <term>Bath <gloss>Latin: Balneo. A water bath (bain-marie) used to ensure the substance does not overheat.</gloss></term>. This must be done slowly in a tempered warmth and not quickly—neither too hot nor too cold.

In this way, the **Stone** rises from the Earth into Heaven—that is, into the **Still-head** <term>Still-head <gloss>Latin: Alembicum. The cap of a distilling vessel where vapors gather.</gloss></term> and then into the receiver—and descends again from Heaven to the Earth. This means the **Body** is separated and then joined together again, as I have said before: the **Spirit** <term>Spirit <gloss>Latin: Spiritus. The volatile or gaseous principle.</gloss></term> and the **Body** must become one single thing.

This occurs through a gentle cooking, kept always at a constant heat, no hotter than a hen sitting to hatch her eggs <note>The "incubation" of the Stone was frequently compared to the hatching of a chick, emphasizing the need for a precise, life-sustaining temperature.</note>. And when this has happened, you will possess the glory and clarity of the whole world. This is the preparation of this **Matter** <term>Matter <gloss>Latin: Materia. The physical substance being transformed.</gloss></term> in the vessel, which is considered to represent the whole world. As the philosopher **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom the foundation of alchemy is attributed.</note> says, this work is called the **Small World** <note>original: "minor Mundus". This refers to the concept of the Microcosm, the idea that the alchemical process mirrors the creation and functioning of the entire universe (the Macrocosm).</note>.

And once this is prepared, you will have the glory and great joy of this secret, which no other man on earth can or may possess. You will also gain the reason and understanding of how the world was created in the beginning; you will understand and learn this through this work. Furthermore, you can drive away all sickness from metals <note>In alchemy, "sick" metals are base metals like lead or tin; "curing" them means transmuting them into perfect gold.</note> and from the human body, and you shall never need to suffer poverty again.

<summary>This page describes the alchemical process of "circular distillation," where the substance is repeatedly vaporized and condensed at a gentle heat (compared to a brooding hen) to unify Spirit and Body. Achieving this "Small World" grants the practitioner health, wealth, and an understanding of the divine creation of the universe.</summary>

# Another little treatise of the same content as the previous one, very useful to read.

## Preface <note>original: "Præfatio"</note>

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial 'E' featuring scrolling foliage and floral motifs.</image-desc>

Everyone might well wonder why the philosophers <term>Philosophers <gloss>In this context, specifically the 'Alchemical Philosophers' or Adepts who claimed to understand the secrets of nature.</gloss></term> have devised so many hidden sayings in honor of this divine and most precious Art—called the *Secret of the Philosophers* <note>original: "Secretum Philosophorum"</note>—in order to conceal it from the unworthy; yet all of this produces nothing fruitful among the disciples of these mysteries <term>Mysteries <gloss>original: "Arcanen," from the Latin 'arcanum,' referring to secret or hidden knowledge, particularly the secrets of nature and alchemy.</gloss></term>, since the authors have been diligent in hiding the truth thereby. And when the ignorant read such writings of theirs and hear what great wealth, virtue, and power <term>Power <gloss>German: "Krafft." In alchemy, this refers to the 'virtue' or intrinsic potency of a substance to effect change or healing.</gloss></term> our Art can bring forth, it gives them such a sweet and lovely sound in their ears; so much so that they think they are already sitting on golden chairs, and that the heavens are full of fiddling <note>The German idiom "der Himmel hängt voller Geigenspiel" suggests a state of unrealistic, blissful optimism.</note>. From this arises such a great lust and desire that they imagine they will suddenly become a Doctor <note>A 'Doctor' here refers to a master or teacher of the alchemical Art.</note>.

<summary>This page introduces a new alchemical treatise, beginning with a preface that critiques the obscure language used by earlier philosophers. It warns that while the promise of alchemical wealth sounds enticing to the "ignorant," the truth remains intentionally hidden from the unworthy.</summary>

<keywords>Secretum Philosophorum, alchemy, hidden knowledge, allegory, alchemical masters, greed vs. wisdom, preface</keywords>

--- Page 255 ---
<meta>This page continues the preface from page 196. The text flows directly from the previous page's description of how the ignorant are led astray by their own fantasies of wealth, concluding the thought that they "imagine they" want to perform great miracles.</meta>

(197)

<sig>N 3</sig>

...imagine they **want to perform great miracles**; they do not believe they are mistaken, nor that the **philosophers** will deceive them. However, they do not know that from the very beginning, the Sages have hidden the foundation of this **Art** <term>Art <gloss>In alchemical texts, "the Art" refers specifically to the practice of alchemy, the "Great Work" of transforming substances.</gloss></term> and have explained the truth only to their own "children" <note>In alchemy, "children" or "sons of the Art" refers to initiated students or those deemed worthy by God to understand the secrets.</note> through **parables** and allegories. 

It is impossible for any person, as long as they may live in this sorrowful world, to fathom the sense and meaning of all the philosophers, much less read all their books and writings with true understanding. Therefore, it is even more impossible to obtain a solid foundation and the fulfillment of one's desire from these same books and writings—unless Almighty **God**, out of special grace and mercy, opens someone's understanding so that they first recognize the property of **Nature**. From this, one may then judge the writings and **sayings** <note>original: "dicta"</note> of the philosophers. For it is **Nature** alone that performs everything, through **Art** in our **mastery** <term>Mastery <gloss>original: "Meisterschafft," referring to the successful completion of the alchemical process or the "Magistery."</gloss></term>; and **Nature** provides the true knowledge of the substance from which we prepare our **Art**. 

For **Bason** <note>Likely referring to a figure from the "Turba Philosophorum," an influential early alchemical text.</note> speaks thus: "Beware that you mix or add nothing foreign to it, for it is one nature that overcomes all things." And **Bond.** <note>Likely Bonellus, another figure from the "Turba Philosophorum."</note> explains this matter thus, calling it a **water**, and says: "The entire work and the whole governance of the work occurs solely through the **water** that comes from the **stone**." And **Alphidius** <note>An alchemist often cited in medieval tracts, whose name is likely a Latinized version of an Arabic author.</note> says: "The **Philosopher's Stone** has within it four..."

<meta>catchword: Na [likely anticipating "Nature" or "Natur" on the following page]</meta>

<summary>This page explains that the "Art" of alchemy is intentionally hidden in parables by the Sages and can only be understood if God grants the seeker an understanding of Nature. It quotes authorities like Bason, Bonellus, and Alphidius to emphasize that the work relies on a single natural substance, often described as "water" from the "stone."</summary>
<keywords>parables, Nature, Bason, Bonellus, water of the stone, Alphidius, divine grace, sons of the art</keywords>

--- Page 256 ---
<meta>This page continues the discourse on the unique nature of the alchemical substance. The catchword from the previous page, "Cör," completes the word "Cörperliche" (corporeal) which introduces the "natures" described at the start of this page.</meta>

(198)

**[Corporeal] natures**, each in its own essence and form, distinct and visible. Such power and strength is found in no other stone on Earth. Therefore, the Royal Philosopher **Haly** <note>likely referring to Khalid ibn Yazid, an early Umayyad prince credited with being one of the first Muslim alchemists.</note> says: "You know no other that may be compared to this stone, which has the power and might to accomplish such things." To this, **Morienes** <note>Morienus, a legendary 7th-century hermit and alchemist, famously the teacher of Khalid ibn Yazid.</note> answers and says: "I have never known any stone that may equal this stone, nor that has such power and effect, for it has within it the **four elements** <gloss>The four fundamental building blocks of matter in ancient and medieval science: Earth, Air, Fire, and Water.</gloss> visibly. Therefore, it is compared to the world and the creatures of this world." Furthermore, one can find in the entire world no stone that may equal it in effect and nature.

Therefore, whoever takes any other thing or any other stone than this aforementioned **Stone of Mastery** <term>Stone of Mastery <gloss>original: "Stein der Meisterschafft," another term for the Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the alchemical process.</gloss></term>, his work is spoiled. As the ancient philosopher **Arros** <note>Likely a reference to a figure from the "Turba Philosophorum" (Assembly of the Philosophers), often thought to be a corruption of the name Rhazes or a similar Arabic authority.</note> says: "The stone is not fit and suitable for our work until one separates from it the **coarse earth**." 

**Morienes** says: "Unless you cleanse the **body** <term>Body <gloss>original: "Cörper," referring to the solid, physical matter or "base" of the substance being transformed.</gloss></term> well of its coarseness, the **spirit** <term>Spirit <gloss>original: "Geist," the volatile or life-giving essence that must be reunited with the purified physical matter.</gloss></term> cannot unite with it. However, as soon as it is purified of its coarse nature, the spirit joins itself to it and rejoices with it, for they have both become subtle and pure from their coarse nature."

**Alcanus** in the *Turba* <note>The "Turba Philosophorum," one of the oldest and most influential Latin alchemical texts.</note>: "The spirits are not united with unclean bodies. But when the..."

<summary>This page emphasizes the unique, elemental composition of the Philosopher's Stone and the absolute necessity of purifying the 'body' of the substance before it can reunite with its 'spirit.'</summary>

<keywords>Haly, Morienes, Stone of Mastery, four elements, purification, spirit and body, Turba Philosophorum, Arros, Alcanus</keywords>

**Bodies** <term>Bodies <gloss>original: "Cörper," referring to the solid material or metallic base in the alchemical process.</gloss></term> are well-digested <note>In alchemy, "digestion" refers to the process of exposing a substance to gentle, continuous heat to bring about a chemical change.</note> and purified/ and then the **Spirit** <term>Spirit <gloss>original: "Geist," the volatile principle or essence that animates the "body."</gloss></term> is joined to them/ in that hour great wonders appear/ for then appear all the colors that are in the world/ and the imperfect body is colored with the permanent color of the **ferment** <term>Ferment <gloss>original: "Ferment," a substance that acts like yeast, transforming and "leavening" the base material into a higher state.</gloss></term>/ for the ferment is its soul/ and the Spirit along with the soul are united and bound to the body/ and are colored together with the body and changed into the color of the ferment/ and from them one thing is made/ therefore the **Philosophers** <note>A term typically referring to the ancient and medieval alchemical authorities.</note> say truly with hidden words/ that it is a joining of the man and the woman/ that is of the Body and the Spirit <note>original: "Corporis & Spirit."</note>/ so that from both of them a fruit may be born/ For as the parents are/ so also shall the children be. ...beginning of all **Natural things**/ and primarily the arrival of the first human/ and likewise his passing away/ speaking thus:

And therefore it is to be seen/ that even today in the first two elements, that is earth and water <note>original: "terra & aqua"</note>, the last two elements are hidden/ and they are fire and air <note>original: "sunt ignis & aër"</note>.
