Immediately set out again to run toward Paradise / and to ask for the Oil of Mercy / and in the meantime, Adam has blessedly passed away / but when Seth arrived before Paradise / and asked for the Oil of Mercy <note>original: "Oleum der Barmherzigkeit"</note> / he was given the little seeds of the Oil Tree by the angel at God’s command / which seeds Seth, upon his return home, planted on his father’s grave / and finally from these seeds grew the wood / on which our Lord Jesus Christ suffered the bitter death for us poor sinful people / and although the Oil was not given to Adam in a physical manner by the angel / and he did not get to enjoy the Wood of Life <note>original: "Holz deß Lebens"</note> here in his mortal time. Nevertheless, Almighty God graced him after his passing / and sprinkled him with the Oil of Mercy / which is to say / that the eternal God has now forgiven him all his sins out of grace and mercy and henceforth for all eternity will never think of them again / nor be angry with him.

Secondly, Almighty God has now also given Adam the spiritual Wood or Bread of Life to eat / and quenched him with the Water of Life <term>aqua vitæ</term> <note>original: "aqua vitæ"; literally "water of life," referring here to the spiritual restoration of the soul in the afterlife</note> / so that Adam shall never again hunger or thirst for all eternity / because he has now finally reached the highest glory and majesty / and henceforth for all eternity he need not fear any adversity / such as hunger, thirst, heat, or frost / nor whatever other such mishaps <term>accidentz</term> <note>original: "accidentz"; a term from natural philosophy referring to external or physical changes that affect a body, like sickness or weather</note> there may be / which we humans encounter in this temporal life / and in summary, he no longer needs to be terrified of death for all eternity / and is therefore now truly clarified / perfect / complete / holy, and righteous before God. Therefore let us consider and rightly recognize that no higher, more secret, or holier mystery <term>secret</term> <note>the author uses "secret" to mean a profound spiritual or alchemical truth hidden from the uninitiated</note> was ever given to the human race by God / as far as human reason is permitted to grasp / than this aforementioned Oil of Mercy <note>original: "Oleum misericordiæ"</note> and Wood of Life <note>original: "lignum vitæ"</note> / which serves us for our soul’s health and salvation / which is then to be praised and boasted of above all other secrets.

<summary>This page describes the legend of Seth obtaining seeds from the Tree of Life for the dying Adam, which eventually grow into the wood of the Cross. It interprets Adam's physical death as a spiritual "clarification" where he is finally freed from mortal suffering through God's mercy.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discourse following the death of Adam. In the same way, there is no higher, more secret, or holier thing that God has ever given to mankind, by which man might protect his needy body from all infirmities and sicknesses in this sorrowful world, and also find relief from all misery and suffering, than by this single means next to God—which is the Secret of the Philosophers <note>original: "Secretum Philosophorum"</note> or medicine of the wise <note>original: "medicina sapientum"</note>. Therefore, it is right that every wise and understanding person should strive for no other things, nor further burden their conscience, except to struggle toward these two aforementioned points as much as is ever possible: first, to turn entirely toward God and His grace, upon which the highest and most distinguished Secret depends; for through this, the inner man—the soul—is preserved in eternal glory, of which I have just briefly spoken according to my humble simplicity.

And this is the true Fountain of the Wise <note>original: "Brunnen der Weisen"</note>, and there is nothing on earth that can be compared to it, except for eternal things. With it, one can protect and deliver the mortal body from accidental sickness in this miserable vale of tears <note>original: "Jammerthal"</note>, and also rescue oneself from all poverty. It can keep one entirely pure, clean, and perfect until their very end, and guard against all evil mishaps <term>accidents</term> <note>original: "accidenten"; in a medical context, these are unexpected symptoms or external afflictions</note>. Furthermore, it can transform the imperfect metallic bodies into perfectly pure and perfected gold and silver <note>original: "sieder"; likely a scribal error for "Silber" (silver)</note>, better than Nature itself may cook it within the earth. Since this is then the highest secret in the world, which behaves, orders, and disposes itself just like the Creation, I have therefore not wished to neglect showing this instruction regarding the Creation.

And I will now, from the true foundation of philosophy, briefly show my consensus <note>original: "Consensum"; agreement or harmony of opinion</note> according to the custom of the philosophers, which corresponds exactly with the creation and generation of man, as all philosophers must give testimony. I will therefore, in the name of the undivided Holy Trinity and for the true benefit of all pious Christian believers, begin to explain the mystery of the scattered sayings of the philosophers; and so, firstly: According to

<meta>This page continues the alchemical allegory of Adam, transitioning from his spiritual state to the physical and chemical principles he represents in the "Great Work." It explains the necessity of separating and recombining elements—symbolized by Adam and Eve—to achieve life and transformation in the laboratory.</meta>

<page-num>212</page-num>
<meta>catchword: dust</meta>

As you have heard what I said regarding the Order of Creation: how God the Almighty Creator filled His Paradise with all kinds of growth—such as herbs, roots, flowers, trees, foliage, and grass as <term>vegetation</term> <note>original: "vegetabilia"</note>, likewise with all <term>animals</term> <note>original: "animalibus"</note> and <term>minerals</term> <note>original: "mineralibus"</note>; and how God the eternal Creator took pleasure in it and spoke His blessing over it, so that each thing had to produce seed and fruit according to its kind.

Only the man Adam alone (that is our <term>Matter</term> <note>original: "Materia"</note>) could produce no fruit by himself. If nature were to complete its course in him so that his lineage might be increased, the inner part had to be separated from him and rejoined to him as his Eve, as was previously indicated. And this is to be understood in no other way than this: when our <term>Matter</term> <note>original: "Mat."</note> is still coarse and whole at the beginning, it cannot bring forth fruit until it is <term>divided</term> <note>original: "dividire"</note>, and the coarse is separated from the subtle, or the water from the earth. The water is accounted as Eve or the Spirit, and the earth as Adam or the <term>Body</term> <note>original: "Corpus"</note>.

And just as the man is dead in terms of procreation until the woman is laid beside him, in the same way, this earth is dead. If it is to become living, the water must be added back to it, from which the earth or <term>Body</term> <note>original: "Corpus"</note> receives its life again. This is what Hermes, the <term>most ancient Philosopher</term> <note>original: "antiquissimus Philosophus"; referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy</note>, speaks of, namely: the dead wishes to be made alive, and the sick healthy. Therefore, it is necessary that you join the corpse and the soul together, and thus the lowermost is turned uppermost—that is, the <term>Body</term> <note>original: "Corpus"</note> becomes Spirit and the Spirit becomes <term>Body</term> <note>original: "Corpus"</note>.

But this is not to be understood as the Spirit becoming the Body on its own, or the Body becoming Spirit on its own; rather, they must be brought together, so that the <term>Spirit</term> <note>original: "Spiritus"</note> as the water dissolves—that is, awakens—the <term>Body</term> <note>original: "Corpus"</note> as the earth. In turn, the Body accepts the Spirit as the water, and they unite with one another to become one <term>Mass</term> <note>original: "Massa"</note>. That is: the earth is softened by the water, and the water is hardened or thickened by the dryness of the earth, just as children’s play in the streets shows, when they take the dry dust

<summary>This page uses the biblical creation of Adam and Eve as an allegory for the chemical process of separation and conjunction. It explains that "Matter" must be divided into Body (earth) and Spirit (water) and then recombined to create life or the alchemical "Mass."</summary>

It uses the metaphor of "child's play" to describe the ease of the work once the principles are understood, while detailing the process of distillation and the "awakening" of the dead matter.</meta>

...dust and moisten it with water, and make a slurry or <term>amalgam</term> <note>original: "amalgama"; a soft, malleable mixture of substances, often referring to a paste made with mercury.</note> out of it. Because of this, the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: "Philosophi"; referring to the ancient alchemists.</note> say that our work is "child's play." Within it, the death of the one is the life of the other; or the hardening of the one makes the other white. And it is nothing other than <term>Body</term> <note>original: "Corpus"</note> and <term>Spirit</term> <note>original: "Spiritus"</note>, and these two are of one nature and of one <term>matter</term> <note>original: "Mater"</note>. Therefore, the philosopher <term>Hermes</term> <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy.</note> says: "O strong nature, overcoming and transcending, and making the settled nature joyful." For water is the strongest nature, which transcends and overcomes, and awakens the settled nature in the <term>Body</term> <note>original: "Corpore"</note>—that is, makes it joyful—of which you shall hear more hereafter. (Do not let the time seem too long to you.)

As it has now been said, Adam (that is, the <term>Body</term> <note>original: "Corpus"</note>) is dead at birth without Eve (that is, without the <term>Spirit</term> <note>original: "Spirit"</note>). Therefore, it should be noted here: when the water is distilled off from our <term>matter</term> <note>original: "Mater"</note>, the <term>Body</term> <note>original: "Corpus"</note> remains lying at the bottom of the <term>flask</term> <note>original: "Kolben"; a glass vessel used for distillation.</note>, dead and sick at its birth, because the <term>Spirit</term> <note>original: "Spiritus"</note> or its soul has been taken from it, withdrawn, and has risen up from it. And the <term>Body</term> <note>original: "Corpus"</note> is black, poisonous, and deadly, as the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: "Philosophi"</note> describe it.

But if one now wishes to awaken the <term>Body</term> <note>original: "Corpus"</note> again, and cleanse it from its blackness and stench so that it may be prepared for <term>procreation</term> <note>original: "generation"</note>, then one must add back to it its withdrawn "sweat"—that is, its <term>Soul</term> <note>original: "Animam"</note> (which is the <term>Spirit</term> <note>original: "Spiritur"</note>, its Eve)—so that the <term>Body</term> <note>original: "Corpus"</note> may receive the soul and be within it, so that she may become pregnant. <term>Senior</term> <note>Senior Zadith, or Zadith ben Hamuel, a 10th-century alchemist.</note> says: the uppermost smoke should be brought to the lowermost smoke, and the Divine Water is a king descending from heaven; it is a restoration of the soul to its corpse, which it makes living after its death.

Note well that a <term>Fixed Salt</term> <note>original: "Fixes Salz"; a salt that remains stable and does not vaporize when heated.</note> is hidden in the <term>Body</term> <note>original: "Corpore"</note>; the same is enclosed within it, just as the <term>male seed</term> <note>original: "Sperma masculinum"</note> was hidden in Adam. The spirit, as Eve, takes this into itself and becomes pregnant from it. This is as much as to say: the seed of the <term>Body</term> <note>original: "Corporis"</note>, which is called the fixed salt, is drawn out of the <term>Body</term> <note>original: "Corpore"</note> by its own water, which has been separated from it.

<summary>This page explains the alchemical process of distillation as a separation of Body and Spirit, followed by their purification and reunion to create "living" matter. ...so that it also becomes subtle and volatile, and rises with the Spirit into the heavens, so that the fixed is then made volatile, or the dead is raised up; and as I have spoken, the settled nature is made joyful and receives life again from its Spirit. Therefore, the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: "Philosophi"; referring to the alchemists of antiquity and the Middle Ages.</note> call it the "Living Water of the Man" <note>original: "lebendige Wasser von dem Manne"</note>, because it is drawn out of the <term>Body</term> <note>original: "Corpore"</note> as if from the man. And the philosopher Lucas commands that one should take the same and cook it according to the custom of nature. Some Philosophers call the Body the "black fruitful earth" for these reasons: because the <term>fixed Salt</term> <note>original: "fixe Salß"; the stable, non-volatile mineral component</note> is invisibly hidden within the Body like a fruit. Others call it the **Black Raven**, in which the White Dove lies hidden, and the water that has been distilled from the Body [is called] <term>Virgin's Milk</term> <note>original: "lac virginis"; a milky-white liquid produced during distillation, often representing the purifying agent.</note>, with which one shall lure the White Dove out of the Black Raven. The Philosophers call this thing by many other innumerable names, as their writings sufficiently show, which need not be mentioned here.

And thus the water is embraced by the Body, and the Seed of the Body—that is, the <term>fixed Salt</term>—works into the water so that it becomes pregnant by it; so that the water dissolves the Body, and in distilling it off again, it takes something of the fixed Salt with it over the <term>helm</term> <note>original: "Helm"; the top part or "head" of a still or alembic.</note>. Through repeated distillation, the water begins to become thicker and thicker. Therefore, much depends upon the <term>reiteration</term> <note>original: "Reiteration"; the repetitive process of pouring a liquid back onto a solid and distilling it again to increase its potency and "fix" the volatile parts.</note>, for the Philosophers praise it and say that one should not let oneself be vexed by the labor. Hermes speaks of it thus:

> When I saw that the water became thick and began to become hard, I rejoiced, for I knew for a truth that I had found what I sought and obtained what I desired.

Therefore, one must pour the water onto its Body and let it <term>digest</term> <note>original: "digeriren"; to subject to gentle, constant heat.</note> so that the Body is dissolved by it, and then draw it off again so that the Body <term>coagulates</term> <note>original: "coagulir"; thickens or solidifies.</note>. In this manner, the Body is well-rubbed, washed, and purified. This must happen as often—with pouring on and drawing off—until all its salt or power is drawn out of the Body. This may be recognized when the water becomes thick and white, and in the...

...coldness, it coagulates <note>original: "coagulirt"; to thicken or solidify from a liquid state.</note> like ice, becoming hard and brittle, and in the heat, it melts like butter or lard, and no longer wishes to dissolve anything from the **Body** <note>original: "Corpore"; the solid material or "salt" at the bottom of the vessel.</note>. Therefore, one should cast away the remaining Body, for it is the superfluous part of the Matter. For this reason, the **Philosophers** <note>The "Philosophi" refers to the ancient and medieval authorities of alchemy.</note> say: "In the preparation, we cast away what is found to be superfluous in it, and from a single thing, we create our **Mastery** <note>original: "Meisterschafft"; referring to the Great Work or the Philosopher's Stone.</note>," where we otherwise add or take away nothing, except, as mentioned, the superfluous. For it has in abundance everything it should have.

And the water, which is called only a "white foliated earth" by the Philosophers, could be taken and cast upon **Quick-silver** <note>original: "Mercurium vivum"; liquid mercury.</note>; it then transforms into good, stable **Silver**.

But something much higher and nobler is hidden in this water, which one can bring forth without any worry or toil. For when this has been brought so far, one need no longer doubt that one's desire will be attained, for the water grows and increases like a child in the mother's womb. Just as the child first grows for a time in the **vessels of the womb** <note>original: "vasa matricis"</note> in the form of **seed** <note>original: "Sperma"</note>, and afterward changes into flesh and blood—that is, into a thicker **Matter**—and only then are the limbs first formed. 

Likewise, this water grows first in its white color, then moved by the natural heat, it grows ever further and changes from one color to another, just as the child [changes] into flesh and blood by the natural heat of its mother. Afterward, when the Matter begins to redden, one sees a great joy; meaning, when the child receives limbs and life, it can then be seen what it is to become. 

Therefore, this secret is hidden in nature and proceeds through nature. And so I have now taught of the joining of the man and the woman—that is, of the **Body** and the **Spirit**—and of the mingling of the water. This is the end of the first composition, where the whiteness of the Matter—the whiteness drawn out of the blackness, that is, out of the Body—is achieved. Nothing more is needed here than patience and time, as **Morienus** <note>A legendary 7th-century hermit and alchemist often cited for his wisdom on the necessity of patience in the Work.</note> speaks of it. This coagulated white water

--- Page 274 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical and allegorical description of the alchemical "Work." The previous page described the preparation of the "Body" and the "White Foliated Earth" which acts as a womb for the growing philosophical child. The catchword from the previous page, "ein Ende" (an end), refers to the conclusion of this specific instruction set on page 216.</meta>

...This is the true white foliated earth <note>original: "terra alba foliata"; a term for the purified, crystalline state of the matter after it has been washed of its impurities.</note>, in which the **Philosopher** bids us to sow our **Gold** and **Silver**, so that they multiply with a hundred-thousandfold fruit. This is also the clear fountain mentioned by the **Count of Trevisan** <note>Bernard Trevisan (1406–1490), an influential alchemical author who wrote the allegory of the "King's Bath" to describe the process of dissolution and perfection.</note>, into which the **King** enters to bathe. And when the King sits in the bath, none of his servants should enter with him; instead, they shall keep the King’s clothes in safe-keeping until the King has dried up the entire fountain. Then the King will make all his servants into lords, so that they will be as mighty in authority, power, and dominion—as well as strength and force—as the King himself was when he first entered the fountain.

But the King is now adorned with three times more dignity than he was before, for he wears a triple crown <note>original: "dreyfaltige Diadema"; symbolizing the perfection of the three alchemical principles: Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury.</note> upon his head. He wears a ruby-red robe, beneath which the shirt of purity is girded with the belt of justice. He is thus an honorable **King of Life**, whose dominion no man in this world can conceive. Therefore, one should provide him with a pure, chaste **Bride**, who shall be born from his own lineage, so that he may know her and work within her, so that many noble children may be born of them both. 

The redness is hidden and contained within the whiteness; one should not force it out <note>original: "aufziehen"; to draw out or intensify too quickly by increasing the heat prematurely.</note> but cook it gently until it becomes perfectly red. Regarding this whiteness, the **Turba** <note>referring to the *Turba Philosophorum* (Assembly of the Philosophers), one of the oldest and most famous Latin alchemical texts.</note> speaks thus: "When you seekers of treasures find the whiteness, be certain that the redness follows the whiteness. For the redness is hidden within the whiteness; one must not force it out, but cook it gently until it becomes red."

With this, an end.

### Hermes Says.

It is necessary to have knowledge of the beginning, both of natural things and of those made by art. For he who does not know the right beginning will not come to the end.

The love of God and of one's neighbor is the perfection of all wisdom.

->To love God is the highest wisdom, and time is our possession. <note>original Latin: "Diligere Deum sapientia summa, & tempus possessio nostra."</note><-

It ends with spiritual and philosophical axioms attributed to Hermes Trismegistus regarding the importance of understanding the origins of the work.</summary>

of several notable Philosophical Writings and Treatises concerning the **Philosopher's Stone** <term>Stein der Weisen; the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold and granting long life</term> / wound and knotted together according to the course of Nature / but now, in these latter days / after the appearance of the **Dawn of Wisdom** <note>original: "Morgenröthe der Weißheit"; likely an allusion to the "Aurora," a common symbol for spiritual and intellectual awakening in 17th-century mystical thought</note> and the commencement of the **Age of Elias the Artist** <note>original: "Seculo Eliæ Artistæ"; a prophetic figure in alchemical lore, expected to appear and reveal all the hidden secrets of nature and art</note> / dissolved and explained for the benefit of the **Sons of the Hermetic Doctrine** <note>original: "filiis Doctrinæ Hermeticæ"; a traditional way of addressing students or initiates of the alchemical tradition attributed to Hermes Trismegistus</note> /
